It opens with our review of the deck (score, reasons, what to keep, what to repair) and then records the deck slide by slide in enough detail to rebuild it: every slide's text verbatim, its layout, every chart with axes, series and values, every photograph and diagram described, colours and typefaces, the position of each element, and speaker notes or a transcript where the source had them. Read this file instead of the PDF, and cite the entry URL below when you refer to the deck or to the review._

## Review

**6 out of 10, Needs substantial work.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a clear route (what you hand off, why a stop line matters, how the product works, what you have at month six) and a consistent dark visual identity with a Continue/Adjust/Stop color code. The readable slides 1, 11 and 18 are well built: slide 11's chart is annotated with a clear takeaway bar, and slide 18 carries one scenario comparison with honest hypothetical labels. But most of the contact sheet is dense, with body text far too small for a screen or room, such as the text-heavy slides 3, 4, 5, 6 and 19, and the product screenshots on 13, 14, 15 and 16 are small, mostly in Japanese and cut off, so an English reader cannot verify the product. The last page (21) is a cramped, differently styled contact panel, and the FAQ and comparison table are tiny with low-contrast text, so the core story can be recovered but substantial revision is needed.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the Continue/Adjust/Stop color system, the strong headline-plus-takeaway structure of slides 8, 9 and 11, and the scenario layout of slide 18 with its explicit hypothetical footnote.

**Most useful repair:** Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.

**What would change the score:** Rework slide 21 to match the deck's visual style and bring the pricing slide 6 into a simple, legible price ladder instead of a dense table with fine print.

_Confidence medium. 21 of 21 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide and table of contents, then moves through four parts: what the client hands off, why a stop line matters, what LOGOTORU OS shows, and what the client has at month six. It first describes the four deliverables (execution, observation design, population definition and a monthly decision report) and a six-month plan split into design, launch, run and decide phases. A customer-voices slide and a track-record section follow, with ORIT's summary marked as the company's own account rather than a quote. Next comes the problem framing: enterprise outbound tends to push without a defined stop threshold, the target list is finite, and negative responses are hard to reverse. Charts on burnout indicators support this argument. The product section shows the manager view, operator view and decision log with screenshots, then a month-six deliverables slide and an illustrative ROI slide marked as hypothetical. A comparison table with alternatives, a FAQ, pricing, founder background and contact details close the deck, with a final call to book a consultation.

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the LOGOTORU enterprise outbound deck have?**

The deck has 21 slides, as shown by the page numbering on slide 1 (01 / 21) and slide 21 (21 / 21).

**What market size figures does the LOGOTORU deck give for Japanese enterprise outbound?**

Slide 9 gives a target of 4,000 accounts in the enterprise tier, marked as about 0.1% of a total of 4M companies (all Japanese corporations). The slide labels both figures as the company's estimates.

**What does LOGOTORU charge per month for the Standard plan in its deck?**

Slide 6 lists the STANDARD plan at ¥1M per month excluding tax, covering call, email and letters up to 100 per month, each tracked by QR.

**How is the LOGOTORU deck structured?**

Slide 2, the table of contents, divides the deck into five parts: I WHAT, II WHY, III HOW, IV VALUE and V NEXT STEPS.

Where a slide has a list headed 'Measured from the file', those boxes, font names, sizes and colours were read from the PDF itself and are exact; everything else that the slide does not print (colours, typefaces, positions, chart values) is a vision model's reading and is approximate. **Layout**

Single full-bleed dark slide with no grid of columns. Left-aligned text block starts at about 5% from the left edge; the logo sits top-left at about 5% from the left and 10% from the top. The headline occupies the middle band with the kicker line above it, and a footer row sits near the bottom with the presenter credit at left and a three-item legend at right. A small running footer 'LOGOTORU OS' at bottom-left and page number '01 / 21' at bottom-right sit near the bottom edge.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background is a very dark navy #0F172A, flat with no gradient. Headline text is bold geometric sans-serif (Inter-like), white #F8FAFC for the first two lines and periwinkle #7C94EE for the third line; headline cap-height is roughly 8.8% of slide height. Kicker line is a widely letter-spaced monospace (Space Mono / JetBrains Mono-like), periwinkle #7C94EE, about 2.2% of slide height, all with wide tracking. Presenter credit is regular sans-serif in slate grey #94A3B8 at about 2.2% of slide height. Legend labels are regular sans-serif #F8FAFC at about 2.0% of slide height, preceded by filled circular dots: green (about #10B981) for Continue, amber (about #F59E0B) for Adjust, red (about #EF4444) for Stop. Running footer and page number are monospace, about 1.6% of slide height, grey #475569-like. Logo is a bold italic/heavy wordmark in white. No shapes, rules or shadows.

### Slide 3

**Review notes**

- Review: But most of the contact sheet is dense, with body text far too small for a screen or room, such as the text-heavy slides 3, 4, 5, 6 and 19, and the product screenshots on 13, 14, 15 and 16 are small, mostly in Japanese and cut off, so an English reader cannot verify the product.
- Suggested repair: Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.

**Text**

What you hand off: execution through improvement
Execution that doesn't stop at the call — we record every response and decide next month's approach. The 4 dimensions we deliver.
TEAM DESIGN
Execution and decision-making are not separated
→ Raw signal flows straight into the decision
The founder, Hashimoto, directs every engagement personally. Execution and
decision-making are never split. Operator is, in principle, fixed (1 person per
account) to maximize learning curve.
OBSERVATION DESIGN
Execution designed to capture observation data
→ "Channels that work / channels that do not" become identifiable
Call and email are the base observation channels, with letters and LinkedIn
added by plan (every response is recorded in LOGOTORU OS). We identify
"which contact point works" by industry, department, and role.
POPULATION DEFINITION
Define the market population required for decision-
making
→ "Who to reach" is structured by name
Using public personnel data, we define the decision-maker universe across
industry × role × company size, and structure it down to named individuals.
DECISION REPORT
Ask every month: should BDR investment continue?
→ Continue / exit, explainable to the board
Every month we report whether to keep investing in this BDR motion. Continue /
stop / adjust — reported at board-ready quality.
LOGOTORU OS
03 / 21

**Layout**

Four equal cards in a 2×2 grid spanning the content area below a left-aligned title and subtitle, with a 5% left and right margin and about 1.3% gutters between cards. The title sits at the top-left at about 10% from the top, the subtitle sits directly beneath it, and the cards occupy roughly 23% to 91% of the slide height. A footer row at the bottom holds the left-aligned wordmark LOGOTORU OS and the right-aligned page number 03 / 21.

**Visuals**

none. The slide has no charts, photographs, illustrations, logos or icons. The only non-text marks are four flat rounded-rectangle card panels, a thin vertical blue accent bar to the left of the title, and four faint short horizontal rules at the top-right of each card.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark navy #0F172A, flat with no gradient, photograph or pattern. Dark navy slide with a flat background. The title is white (#F8FAFC) in a heavy geometric sans-serif (Inter Bold style, tight tracking) at about 6% of slide height. Subtitle and body copy are cool grey (#94A3B8) in Inter Regular, body at about 1.9% of slide height and subtitle at about 2.1%. Eyebrow labels are periwinkle blue (#7C94EE) in a monospace face (JetBrains Mono style, medium weight, all capitals, wide letter-spacing) at about 1.7% of height. Card headings are white (#F8FAFC) Inter SemiBold at about 2.8% of height. Arrow lines are periwinkle (#7C94EE) Inter SemiBold at about 2.1%. Cards are filled #1E293B with a 1px white border at 10% opacity and small corner radii of about 8px; no shadows or gradients. The title accent bar is a deep blue (#1E40AF). The decorative rules are a very dim blue-grey (#1E3A8A at low opacity).

### Slide 4

**Review notes**

- Review: But most of the contact sheet is dense, with body text far too small for a screen or room, such as the text-heavy slides 3, 4, 5, 6 and 19, and the product screenshots on 13, 14, 15 and 16 are small, mostly in Japanese and cut off, so an English reader cannot verify the product.
- Suggested repair: Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.

**Text**

FIRST 6 MONTHS
The first 6 months: who does what
01 Design
Contract day to week 2
US
Fix the target market and what counts as a meeting
Build the decision-maker list and BDR strategy report
60-min walkthrough: which industries to start with
Write the call script and letter copy
YOU
Share existing customers and no-go accounts; approve the list
YOU GET
Decision-maker list / BDR strategy report / copy
02 Launch
Week 3 to month 1
US
Your dedicated operator starts calls, email and letters
Every response logged by industry and role, daily
A weekly 30-min session to adjust the approach
First verdict at the 30-day review
YOU
Run the meetings and tell us how they went
YOU GET
The first monthly decision report
03 Run
Months 2 to 6
US
Reach 150–250 people a month
Continue / shrink / stop, judged per industry
60-min review to set next month’s allocation
Carry what worked into next month
YOU
Join the review; approve the allocation
YOU GET
Monthly decision reports / decision log
04 Decide
Month 6
US
Review all six months; recommend continue / change / exit
If we recommend exit, there is no early-termination fee
YOU
Decide to renew, change or end
YOU GET
Contact logs, playbooks and verdict history (yours to keep)
Execution is done by our dedicated operator; your part is mostly review and approval. The initial design (list, strategy report, 60-min walkthrough) is / included in the monthly fee. No setup fee.
LOGOTORU OS
04 / 21

**Layout**

Four equal columns of cards sit side by side beneath a left-aligned eyebrow and title, with the title block starting at about 5% from the left edge. Each card spans y 22% to 80.6% and is separated by equal gutters of about 1.8%; a full-width banner sits below the cards at y 82%, and the footer runs along the bottom with the left label at x 1.2% and the page number right-aligned to x 98.6% at y 96.9%.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background: Solid dark navy #0F172A. Dark navy slide with near-white headings and body text, periwinkle accents for the eyebrow, card labels, numbers and result lines, and slate grey for the timeframe and YOU labels. Titles are a bold geometric grotesque sans-serif (close to Inter Semibold) and the labels, numbers and footer are a monospace (close to JetBrains Mono). Title is about 6% of slide height and body text about 1.9%. Cards are #1E293B with a 1px white 10% opacity outline and small corner radii of about 1.2% of slide width; the banner uses the same fill and radius. Thin #334155 divider rules separate each card's US section from its YOU section. No shadows or gradients.

### Slide 5

**Review notes**

- Review: But most of the contact sheet is dense, with body text far too small for a screen or room, such as the text-heavy slides 3, 4, 5, 6 and 19, and the product screenshots on 13, 14, 15 and 16 are small, mostly in Japanese and cut off, so an English reader cannot verify the product.
- Suggested repair: Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.

**Layout**

Five equal rounded cards sit in one row across the content width (x 5% to 95%), starting at y 23.7% and ending at y 79.3%. The title sits top-left at x 5.9%, y 10%, with a blue accent bar at its left edge and the subtitle beneath it at y 19.7%. A centred letter-spaced caption 'Customers and partners' sits at y 81.1%, above a single row of eight white logo boxes (y 84% to 92%, x 5% to 85%), which are left-aligned rather than spanning full width. Footer text 'LOGOTORU OS' is at bottom-left (x 1.2%, y 96.9%) and the page number '05 / 21' at bottom-right (x 94%, y 96.9%). Left margin is about 5%, right margin about 5%.

**Visuals**

Five customer cards, each with a green monospace tag pill, a category label, a bold headline with an arrow, a large blue opening quotation mark (except card 5), a grey quote or summary paragraph, a thin divider, and a circular headshot photograph with name and role. Headshots: card 1 photograph of Ms. Ichikawa, a woman in a dark jacket seated at a desk, wider crop; card 2 photograph of Mr. Kawai, a man in a dark jacket and light shirt, head and shoulders, dark background; card 3 photograph of Mr. Kaneko, a man in a dark suit and tie, head and shoulders, dark background; card 4 photograph of a man in a dark suit against a grey background, head and shoulders; card 5 photograph of Mr. Ishikawa, a man in a dark suit with arms folded, head and shoulders, dark background. All headshots are cropped to circles, lit softly from the front, with dark, muted colours. Below the cards is a row of eight white rounded logo boxes: 1 Life is Tech! (multicolour pinwheel mark with grey wordmark 'Life is Tech!'), 2 ORIT (grey stacked-stripe emblem with bold black 'ORIT.' wordmark), 3 LEGALSCAPE (grey and navy wordmark with tagline 'WE EMPOWER LEGAL PROFESSIONALS'), 4 Forcepoint (black wordmark with a small square dot mark), 5 Mazrica (orange lifebuoy icon with orange 'Mazrica' wordmark), 6 Panalyt (teal script wordmark), 7 Xbit (black X glyph with 'Xbit' wordmark), 8 SpeakBUDDY (multicolour dots icon with dark 'SpeakBUDDY' wordmark). No charts, tables or diagrams.

**Design**

Background: Solid #0F172A dark navy, full bleed. Dark navy slide with white headline text, slate grey body text and emerald green tags. Title is a bold geometric sans-serif (Inter-like), about 6% of slide height with tight tracking; subtitle and body are regular Inter-like sans-serif at about 2% and 1.8% of slide height. Tags and footer use a monospace face (JetBrains Mono-like). Cards are #1E293B with a 1px white 10% opacity border and corner radius about 12px, no shadow. Tag pills are #10B981 at 15% opacity with fully rounded ends. Accents: blue #1E3A8A quotation marks, blue #1E40AF title bar. Logo boxes are white with about 6px corner radius and a faint white 10% outline halo.

### Slide 6

**Review notes**

- Review: But most of the contact sheet is dense, with body text far too small for a screen or room, such as the text-heavy slides 3, 4, 5, 6 and 19, and the product screenshots on 13, 14, 15 and 16 are small, mostly in Japanese and cut off, so an English reader cannot verify the product.
- Suggested repair: Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.
- What would change the score: Rework slide 21 to match the deck's visual style and bring the pricing slide 6 into a simple, legible price ladder instead of a dense table with fine print.

**Text**

**Layout**

Two columns below a title: the left column (about 5% to 57% of width) stacks four rounded cards from y 24% to y 91%, and the right column (about 60% to 95%) holds one tall pricing card from y 24% to y 96%. The title sits top-left at about y 10% with a thin vertical blue bar to its left; section labels sit above each column at y 19.6%, left-aligned to the column edges. Left margin is 5%, right margin about 5%, and a footer runs along the bottom with 'LOGOTORU OS' at bottom-left and the page number '06 / 21' at bottom-right.

**Visuals**

None. The slide has no chart, table, photograph, illustration, logo or icon. Decorative elements only: four card shapes with thin coloured left accent bars (blue, amber, green, and none on the fourth card), one pricing panel with thin horizontal dividers (solid between Light, Standard and Premium; dashed between Premium and In-house and below In-house), and one tinted callout box for the zero setup fee.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark navy #0F172A. Dark navy background #0F172A with no gradient or texture. Cards are filled #1E293B with a 10% white outline, corner radius about 1% of slide width, no shadow. Titles are near-white #F8FAFC in a bold geometric sans-serif (Inter-like), about 6.0% of slide height. Body text is slate grey #94A3B8 in regular sans-serif (Inter-like) at about 2.0% of slide height. Section labels and plan names are periwinkle #7C94EE in a bold monospace face (JetBrains Mono-like), widely letter-spaced, about 1.7% of slide height. Prices are bold near-white sans about 4.2% of slide height. Accent bars: blue #1E40AF on the first card, amber #F59E0B on the second, green #34D399 on the third. Callout box fill #1E3A8A at 10% opacity with a #1E3A8A outline at 30% opacity and small rounded corners. Footer text is monospace, spaced, about 1.6% of slide height.

### Slide 7

**Text**

About me
Shuhei Hashimoto
橋本 周平
Relationship LLC
Managing Partner / Head of LOGOTORU
▪ Background
Enterprise BDR at Salesforce and AWS.
▪ Track record
Hit quota every quarter at both Salesforce and AWS. At Salesforce, secured 50 meetings with director- / level-and-above decision makers in about a year. Earlier, launched an inside sales team and managed / 20+ partner reps.
▪ Why I founded LOGOTORU
I repeatedly saw enterprise outbound motions exhaust the market faster than expected. The need to / make continue / stop decisions on data — not gut feel — is why LOGOTORU exists.
LOGOTORU OS
07 / 21

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a 16:9 slide. The title 'About me' sits top-left at about y 10%, with a vertical blue accent bar to its left, starting at the left margin of about 5%. The left column (x 5% to 31%) holds a portrait photo from y 19% to 77%, with the name, Japanese name and two affiliation lines stacked beneath it. The right column (x 33.8% to 95%) holds three stacked cards of equal height (23.6% each) with 4.6% vertical gaps, top-aligned with the photo at y 19.1% and ending at y 91.1%. All text is left-aligned. The footer sits at y 96.9%: 'LOGOTORU OS' at the left margin and the page number '07 / 21' right-aligned to about 98.6%. No footnote or logo.

**Visuals**

One photograph: a head-and-shoulders to waist-up portrait of a smiling man with short dark hair, wearing a navy blue textured blazer over a plain white crew-neck T-shirt, with his arms folded across his chest. He is centred slightly left of the frame, facing the camera. The background is a plain dark slate-grey textured wall. Lighting is soft, diffuse and frontal, with even light on the face and no hard shadows. Dominant colours: slate grey, navy blue, white skin and white shirt. The photo is boxed, not full-bleed, with rounded corners, and sits within a faint white 10% outline card. Nothing is cropped at the face or arms. No charts, tables, icons or logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid deep navy #0F172A, flat with no gradient, pattern or photograph behind the content. Dark, flat, tech-style slide. Background is solid deep navy #0F172A. Titles and names are near-white #F8FAFC in a bold geometric sans-serif (Inter Bold or similar) with tight spacing. Body text is cool grey #94A3B8 in a regular-weight Inter-like sans-serif. Accents are a bright blue vertical bar (about #1E3A8A to #2B4BB8) beside the title and indigo bullet squares #7C94EE. Title is about 6.0% of slide height; card headings about 2.4%; body about 2.1%; name about 3.2%. Cards use fill #1E293B at 60% opacity with a 10% white hairline border and rounded corners of about 1% of slide width; no shadows or gradients. The footer uses a letter-spaced monospace face. The file also contains two full-slide white fills, hidden beneath the navy fill.

### Slide 8

**Review notes**

- Worth keeping: Keep the Continue/Adjust/Stop color system, the strong headline-plus-takeaway structure of slides 8, 9 and 11, and the scenario layout of slide 18 with its explicit hypothetical footnote.

**Text**

PROBLEM
"Push harder" is always said.
No one is empowered to say, "Stop here."
Enterprise outbound is structured to only step on the accelerator. Stop/pullback
decisions are run on instinct — and by the time you notice, you've burned the market
itself.
No threshold and no authority to say
"stop"
You can't explain a pullback in numbers, so you
can't stop. Continue / adjust / stop is gut-feel;
replace the person, replace the answer.
You keep pushing a market that no
longer responds
Prospects fatigue; brand impression erodes. A
burned market does not come back.
When to pull back is never recorded
When, where, and why the market started to
slip — no one can explain. The decision history
simply doesn't exist.
LOGOTORU OS
08 / 21

**Layout**

Left-aligned single-column header block with three equal-width cards in one row below it. The PROBLEM kicker sits top-left at about 8.5% from the top, the headline starts about 23.7% down, and the subtitle follows at about 40.9%. The three cards span from 57.5% to 82.1% of the height, with 5% side margins and gaps of about 1.8% between them. The footer label sits bottom-left and the page number bottom-right at about 97% of the height; there is no logo and no slide title in the header area beyond the headline.

**Visuals**

No photographs, charts, icons or logos. The only non-text graphics are three identical rounded card panels in a row, each filled #1E293B with a faint white 10% outline, and each holding a bold heading and a grey body paragraph.

**Design**

Background: Solid deep navy #0F172A, flat with no gradient, photograph or pattern. Dark, flat, text-led slide. Background #0F172A (deep navy). Headline text #F8FAFC (near white) with the phrase "Stop here." in periwinkle #7C94EE. Kicker PROBLEM in #7C94EE. Subtitle and card body text #94A3B8 (slate grey). Card headings #F8FAFC. Typeface for headline, subtitle and card text is Inter (neo-grotesque sans-serif): headline bold, subtitle and body regular, card headings semibold. Kicker and footer are in a monospace face (JetBrains Mono style), kicker bold with wide letter-spacing. Headline is about 6.0% of slide height, card headings about 2.3%, body about 2.0%, kicker about 2.0%, footer about 1.6%. Cards have small corner radii (about 1.2% of width), a 1px outline at white 10% opacity, and no shadow or gradient. Footer text is measured as #FFFFFF but appears dimmed to grey, which suggests reduced opacity.

**Text**

STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINT
The list is finite. There is no replacement.
Of all Japanese corporations, the enterprise tier (publicly listed enterprise tier) is a tiny minority.
TARGET
4,000 accounts
Enterprise tier / ~0.1% (our estimate)
TOTAL
4M companies
All Japanese corporations (our estimate)
CRITICAL RISK
List exhaustion is irreversible
Unlike SMB, once the list is exhausted you cannot just "buy the next list".
A market once lost does not easily come back.
16%
Recovery after a negative response — only 12 of 74 rejection / defensive responses turned positive at the next contact (LOGOTORU OS records). More than 80% did not come back
LOGOTORU OS
09 / 21

Two shapes and one badge. (1) Outlined triangle, left column, apex at about x 26.3% y 52.4%, base corners at about x 20.1% and 32.6% at y 68.3%. (4) A circular badge about 3.1% wide, centred near x 57.8% y 71.5%, filled dark red (about #3B1D27) and containing the text 16%. No photographs, charts or logos.

### Slide 10

**Text**

MARKET BURNOUT RISK
Sloppy outbound burns the market — for good
"Sloppy approach" accumulates negative sentiment and becomes hard to reverse (only 16% turned positive after a
negative response, n=74)
High-volume activity without strategy
"Spray and pray" tactics / low-quality talk tracks
STEP 1
Negative sentiment accumulates
"Pushy" and "annoying" brand impressions stack across the market
STEP 2
Locked out of the market
For years afterward, you can no longer approach that market — an irreversible state
STEP 3
LOGOTORU OS
10 / 21

**Layout**

Left-aligned single column with a 5% left and right margin. Kicker label sits at top left, the large title below it with a red vertical accent bar at its left edge, then a bold lead sentence, then three full-width stacked cards (90% of slide width) joined by downward triangle arrows centred at x 49%. Footer wordmark sits bottom left and page number bottom right, both at about 97% of slide height.

**Visuals**

Three stacked rounded rectangular cards, each with a label and a sub-line, linked by two downward solid triangle arrows (#94A3B8), with a right-aligned step tag on each card. Card 1 is slate (#1E293B fill, white 10% overlay) with a neutral step tag; Card 2 is a dark charcoal tinted amber (#F59E0B at 10% fill, 30% border) with an amber title; Card 3 is a dark maroon tinted red (#EF4444 at 10% fill, 40% border, plus a 30% red outer halo) with a red title. Three small icons are placed at the left of the cards at x 6.6%: a lightning bolt ⚡ in card 1, a pensive face 😞 in card 2 and a no-entry sign 🚫 in card 3; these glyphs are present in the file but render as blank space in the image, so they are not visibly shown. No photographs, charts or logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark navy #0F172A.

### Slide 11

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A dual-line illustration with an annotated three-month lag makes the leading-indicator argument visible at a glance, and it is labelled as illustrative rather than measured data.
- Review: The readable slides 1, 11 and 18 are well built: slide 11's chart is annotated with a clear takeaway bar, and slide 18 carries one scenario comparison with honest hypothetical labels.
- Worth keeping: Keep the Continue/Adjust/Stop color system, the strong headline-plus-takeaway structure of slides 8, 9 and 11, and the scenario layout of slide 18 with its explicit hypothetical footnote.

**Text**

Are you watching the leading indicators of burnout? / From cross-client data — a rise in negative response rate has tended to be followed, about 3 months later, by a drop in conversation success rate (correlation between the negative rate 3 months earlier and this month's conversation success rate: r = −0.69, 26 months). / Negative response rate (leading) / Conversation success rate (lagging) / Illustration (not measured values) / ① Spike / 3-month lag / 40% / 30% / 20% / ② Collapse / 10% / M1 / M4 / M7 / M10 / M12 / ① Negative response rate spikes (leading indicator) / ② Conversation success rate falls (lagging, ~3 months later) / "This month's negative rate" tends to show up in "the conversation rate 3 months from now". / By the time meeting count or revenue drops, the market is already worn down. / LOGOTORU OS / 11 / 21

**Layout**

Single column, full-width, left-aligned title and subtitle with a 5% left and right margin, sitting on a 5% to 95% content width. The title sits at the top (y about 10%) with a thin vertical accent bar to its left. Below it, a large rounded chart card spans y 27% to 77%, containing a legend row at the top, a centred line chart, and a caption legend at the bottom. A full-width callout box sits at y 79% to 91%, with centred text. The footer has a wordmark at bottom-left and a page number at bottom-right, both at y about 97%.

**Visuals**

One line chart, titled by the slide headline and labelled 'Illustration (not measured values)'. Plot area about x 27.5% to 72%, y 34% to 70% of the slide. Y-axis: percent, 0% to about 40%, with labelled gridlines at 10%, 20%, 30% and 40% (0% at the baseline is implied but not labelled). X-axis: months M1 to M12, labelled at M1, M4, M7, M10 and M12. Series 1, 'Negative response rate (leading)', red-coral #F87171 line with dot markers, values about M1 5%, M2 6%, M3 7%, M4 9%, M5 14%, M6 24%, M7 36%, M8 38%, M9 35%, M10 30%, M11 26%, M12 24%. Series 2, 'Conversation success rate (lagging)', blue #60A5FA line with dot markers, values about M1 18%, M2 19%, M3 18%, M4 17%, M5 17%, M6 16%, M7 14%, M8 11%, M9 6%, M10 4%, M11 4%, M12 5%. Values are estimates because the slide prints none. Highlights: a translucent pink-grey band (#F87171 at 10% opacity) behind M5 to M7 labelled ① Spike, and a translucent blue band (#60A5FA at 10% opacity) behind M8 to M10 labelled ② Collapse. A dashed amber #F59E0B arrow runs horizontally at about 40% from about M5 to M9 with a solid triangular head pointing right, labelled '3-month lag'. Legend: a coral pill marker and a blue pill marker at top left. Caption legend at bottom: a red ① and blue ② with text as listed. Gridlines are thin #334155 horizontal lines. No photograph, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid #0F172A dark navy. Dark navy slide with a slate card, a red-tinted callout and white/grey type. Background #0F172A. Card #1E293B with a faint white overlay at 10% opacity. Titles #F8FAFC (near white). Subtitle lead-in #94A3B8 (slate grey) and bold body #F8FAFC. Chart labels #64748B and #94A3B8. Accents: coral #F87171 for the negative series and spike, blue #60A5FA for the success series and collapse, amber #F59E0B for the lag arrow, navy #1E3A8A for the title accent bar. Typeface: Inter (neo-grotesque sans-serif), bold weight for the title and callout, regular for body. Monospace for the axis labels, footer wordmark and page number. Title about 6% of slide height, body about 2.2%, chart labels about 1.3% to 1.6%, footer about 1.6%. Card has rounded corners (about 1.5% radius) with a 1px #334155 border. Callout box has a #1A1020 to #1C1427 dark fill tinted red at 5% opacity, with a #EF4444 border at 30% opacity and rounded corners. No shadows or gradients. The file also contains two full-slide #FFFFFF fills beneath the #0F172A fill, which are not visible.

### Slide 12

**Review notes**

- Suggested repair: Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.

**Text**

What LOGOTORU is
OS A decision operating system that lets enterprise outbound teams explain "continue / adjust / stop"
WHAT WE DON'T DO
We do not declare the "right answer"
Calling the "correct answer" for a market or industry is not our role
×
We do not claim to know the future
No demand or win-rate forecasts. We estimate the risk of staying the / course from what we observe now
×
We do not make the decision for you
Final decision is the executive's. LOGOTORU supplies the evidence
×
We do not guarantee meeting count
Maximizing decision quality — not meeting count — is the goal
WHAT WE DO (CORE VALUE)
01
Assemble the facts (logs) needed for decisions
Not fragmented activity history — structured data shaped for / decision-making.
02
Preserve the reasoning over time
Hypothesis → response → interpretation → decision. We save the / "why we thought so" context.
03
Make decisions auditable in hindsight
Not outcome-only reporting — verify whether the decision was / sound at the time it was made.
LOGOTORU OS
12 / 21

**Layout**

Single slide with a large left-aligned title at top-left (x 5.9%, y 10%), a full-width rounded banner beneath it (x 5%, y 19.1%, w 90%, h 8.1%), and two equal-height columns below: a red-tinted 'What we don't do' panel on the left (x 5%, w 44.1%) and a green-tinted 'What we do' panel on the right (x 50.9%, w 44.1%), both spanning y 28.4% to 91.1%. Each column has a monospace caps header at the top and stacked cards beneath, all left-aligned to a common inner margin of about 1.4% of width. The footer sits at y 96.9%: 'LOGOTORU OS' at the left margin and '12 / 21' right-aligned at the right margin. No logo.

**Visuals**

No photographs, charts, tables or diagrams. Visual elements are icons only: seven small red cross marks (×, #EF4444) at the left edge of the four 'don't do' cards (four icons), and three large green monospace numerals 01, 02, 03 (#10B981) at the left of the three 'core value' cards. Cards are flat rectangles with thin tinted outlines; no shadows or gradients.

**Design**

Background: Solid navy #0F172A. Dark, flat, single-colour navy background (#0F172A) with no texture. Title in bold Inter-style grotesque sans-serif, #F8FAFC, very large (about 6% of slide height). Body text in Inter-style regular sans-serif, #94A3B8 for descriptions and #F8FAFC for card headings, about 2.3% of slide height for headings and 1.8% to 1.9% for descriptions. Accent colours: blue #1E3A8A title bar, periwinkle #7C94EE for the OS tag and highlighted quoted phrase, coral red #EF4444/#F87171 for the 'don't do' column, emerald #10B981/#34D399 for the 'do' column. Section headers and footer use a monospace typeface (JetBrains Mono-style) in wide-tracked capitals. Shapes have small corner radii (about 0.5% of slide width), thin 1px-style outlines at about 20% opacity, and no shadows.

### Slide 13

**Review notes**

- Suggested repair: Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.

**Text**

Why we can do this
DATA FOUNDATION
A proprietary data foundation built for enterprise outbound
(monthly verdict on 5 indicators + analysis by industry, role, and channel)
We structure the qualitative data — conversation analytics and more — that traditional SFA/CRM cannot capture, and deliver it as a decision-making dashboard.
Verdict by Industry
Continue / reduce / stop for each industry, one screen
Contact Record per Person
Which number and channel actually reached them
Where to focus next month
Continue / Reduce / Stop by industry
Recommended channel
Verified numbers + reach record
Connection hint
LOGOTORU OS
13 / 21
(Text inside the two UI screenshots is described under visuals.)

**Layout**

Single-column title block at top left with a short blue vertical accent bar to the left of the title, a full-width rounded card beneath it spanning 5% to 95% of the width, then two equal columns (left 5% to 48.6%, right 51.4% to 95%) each with a small caps heading, a grey subtitle line and a framed screenshot panel. Text is left aligned; the margins are about 5% left and right. The footer label LOGOTORU OS sits bottom left and the page number 13 / 21 sits bottom right, both at about 97% of the height.

**Visuals**

Two framed UI screenshots of dark-mode software, with no photographs. Left screenshot (1470 by 685 px, placed at x 5.1%, y 50.8%, w 43.5%, h 36.0%, inside a dark panel): a table titled 今月の勝ち筋 — どこに寄せるか with subtitle 量を増やすより、刺さっている層に接触を寄せる。無駄打ちは市場を焼く。 and filter chips 業種 / チャネル / 部署 (探索) / 役職 (探索) (first chip active in blue). Below is a line 来月の寄せ先: 寄せる 情報・通信業・小売業 絞小・止める 機械・医薬品・サービス業・証券、商品先物取引業 (寄せる in green, 絞小・止める in red), then a note stating the verdict rule by market NO (5% or more = 縮小, 20% or more = 止める, fewer than 30 people contacted in the last 90 days = 判定保留). Table columns: 業種 / 判定 / 市場NO / 会話成功率 / 商談化率 / パイプ / 直近90日 接触人数 / 未接触社数. Rows: 情報・通信業, 続ける (green badge), 4.0%, 14.0%, 14.0%, ¥80.0M, 56, 3. 小売業, 続ける (green badge), 3.0%, 11.0%, 11.3%, pipeline value hidden behind callout, 55, 3. 機械, 縮小 (orange badge), 12.0%, 6.2%, 6.2%, ¥20.0M, 52, 1. 医薬品, 止める (red badge), 22.0%, 0.0%, –, –, 80, 2. サービス業, 止める (red badge), –, –, –, –, 30, 2. 証券、商品先物…, 止める (red badge), –, –, –, –, 42, 0. Footer row: ▸ 全業種を見る（他4業種 — 判定保留・未接触を含む）. The table is a dark rule-separated grid with grey row lines. Right screenshot (1220 by 670 px, placed at x 52.6%, y 50.8%, w 41.1%, h 40.2%, cropped to its dark window frame): a contact card with 株式会社ホールディングス商事 and link ↗ アカウント詳細を見る, name 田中 英樹 with 課長, subline 経営企画部 · Layer 4, status badge 状態: アクティブ [?] in green, and a close X at top right. Four tiles: 最終ステータス 日程調整中; 最終アプローチ日 2026-09-18 (13日前); 前回チャネル 手紙; 推奨チャネル 手紙 (highlighted with blue outline). A panel 確認済みの番号 lists 代表 [blurred number] コピー; 代表 [blurred number] with a green badge 通信前 (8/9) and コピー; 部署・情報システム部 [blurred number] with a red badge 繋がらない (5回) and コピー. A panel 接続のヒント（参考） shows a green-bordered line 過去 2026-09-18 に本人接触成功 (手紙)。同条件で再現性高い and the note ※ 会話の中身・口説き方は BDR の判断。これは接続経路の参考のみ。 Five dark-blue callout tags overlay the screenshots: Where to focus next month (over the left table header), Continue / Reduce / Stop by industry (over the left table rows), Recommended channel (over the right tile), Verified numbers + reach record (over the right verified-numbers panel), and Connection hint (over the right hint panel). The screenshots are flat, with no photographic lighting, and the dominant colours are navy, slate and white text with green, orange and red status badges.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark navy #0F172A (a full-slide #FFFFFF fill is also present but is covered). Dark navy slide with white and light-blue type. Title is a bold geometric sans (Inter Bold or similar), #F8FAFC, about 6.0% of slide height. The eyebrow DATA FOUNDATION is a bold monospace (JetBrains Mono or similar), uppercase, widely letter-spaced, #7C94EE, about 1.7%. The headline line is a regular sans, #F8FAFC, about 2.7%, with the phrase built for enterprise outbound in semibold #7C94EE. Subtitle and body text are regular sans in slate #94A3B8, about 2.0 to 2.3%. Column headings Verdict by Industry and Contact Record per Person are monospace, letter-spaced, #7C94EE, about 1.8%, with grey sans subtitles about 1.85%. The title is preceded by a vertical bar in #1D4ED8. The card is #1E293B with a white 10% overlay, a thin light border, and corner radius about 1% of width. Panels are white at 5% opacity with a 10% border and rounded corners. Callout tags are #0F172A at 80% opacity with black 10% drop shadows and #1E3A8A text. No gradients.

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The manager view leads with a single verdict badge at the top, so the monthly decision is readable before any supporting metrics.
- Suggested repair: Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.

**Text**

PRODUCT — MANAGER
What a decision looks like — Manager view
One screen for "this month's decision." CONTINUE / ADJUST / STOP with the reasoning and the next action attached.
2026-09 の判定（確定値）
変える
ADJUST
攻めるが、攻め方を変える
主因: 市場NO 10.2% (+3.6pt)。判定の根拠: 健全性スコア 11/15 が 12 未満

今日決めること: 未回答の質問 1 件（医薬品(業種)の停止を確定しますか？）
Verdict
Driver
Today's decision
Hero badge
Verdict, first
CONTINUE / ADJUST / STOP at the top (NO / VERDICT when the month has too little data — 4 / states). The verdict for this month is visible without / scrolling.

**Layout**

Single column on a dark slide with a left-aligned kicker, title and subtitle stacked at the top, all starting at about 5% left margin. A wide hero screenshot panel spans about 5% to 95% width from 26.8% to 68.1% height, followed by a row of three equal cards (each about 29% wide, gaps of about 0.4% between cards... spacing about 30.5%) at 69.4% to 91.1% height. Footer line sits at about 97% height with 'LOGOTORU OS' at the left margin and the slide number '14 / 21' right-aligned near the right margin.

**Visuals**

One UI screenshot inside the hero panel (photo/illustration type): a dark navy product screen with Japanese text showing a verdict. At top left a blue concentric-circle target icon sits beside the heading '2026-09 の判定（確定値）' in white. Below it a large solid amber/orange rounded rectangle (#F59E0B-like, about 0.2% corner radius scaled) contains the white word '変える' in heavy Japanese type, followed by grey letterspaced 'ADJUST' and amber text '攻めるが、攻め方を変える'. Next are three lines of body text: a grey '主因:' label then white text, an orange warning-triangle line, and a grey '今日決めること:' label with white text. The screenshot is clipped at its bottom edge around 68% height by the card row, with rounded top corners and a faint blue inner glow border. Lighting is flat, even UI lighting; the composition is a full-width crop of the screen with the right side empty. Three small tag labels overlay the right side of the screenshot: a green 'Verdict' pill (top right), a dark navy 'Driver' pill (middle right) and a dark navy 'Today's decision' pill (lower right) with blue-navy text. The three cards below contain only text, no icons or charts. Dominant colours: navy #0F172A and #1E293B, amber #F59E0B, green #059669, blue #7C94EE.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark navy #0F172A. Dark navy background #0F172A with no gradient or pattern. Title and card headings are off-white #F8FAFC in a heavy geometric sans-serif (Inter Bold, tight tracking), about 6% of slide height for the title. Kicker and card labels are monospace uppercase or lowercase with wide letterspacing in periwinkle #7C94EE. Body and subtitle text is grey #94A3B8 in regular-weight sans-serif (Inter Regular), about 1.9 to 2.2% of slide height. Key words CONTINUE / ADJUST / STOP are bold #F8FAFC. Accent bar is a vertical blue #1E40AF rule to the left of the title. Cards are filled #1E293B with a white 10% overlay and a thin white 10% border, corner radius about 1%, no shadow. The hero panel uses the same fill with a faint blue #3B82F6 inner glow border and rounded corners about 1.5%.

**Layout**

Single-column header over a two-column body on a dark navy slide. The eyebrow label and two-line title sit flush left at a 5% margin; the title spans nearly the full width below the eyebrow. Two equal columns (left x 5 to 48.9%, right x 51.1 to 95%) each carry a small monospace label, a bold sans heading, a grey two-line description, and a rounded screenshot panel (y 42.3 to 78.2%). A full-width rounded callout panel (y 79.4 to 91.1%) sits below. Footer text 'LOGOTORU OS' sits bottom-left and the page number '15 / 21' bottom-right at about 97% height.

**Visuals**

Two UI screenshots, both boxed in rounded dark panels, and six small dark label pills used as callout backgrounds. Left screenshot (x 7.6, y 42.4, w 38.8, h 35.7; 1600 by 830 px): a dark-mode list titled 'TOP 5 — 今日まず攻めるべき5名' with two stacked contact cards. Card #1 has a blue left border, rank '#1' in blue, name 田中 英樹 / 課長, a status tag 日程調整中 in red-outline, a blue filled button '記録する' with '詳細' and 'AIメール' links, and a score of 65.6 pt at top right. Card #2 has rank '#2', name 小林 愛 / 部長, status tag 未接触, a score of 62.5 pt, and the same button row, partly covered by the 'Next Action' label. Phone numbers and some fields are blurred. Right screenshot (x 54.8, y 42.4, w 36.6, h 35.7; 1220 by 670 px): a person detail view for 田中 英樹 課長 with an 'X' close icon at top right; four stat tiles (最終ステータス 日程調整中, 最終アプローチ日 2026-09-18 (13日前), 前回チャネル 手紙, and 推奨チャネル 手紙 in a blue-outlined highlighted tile); a 確認済みの番号 section listing three rows of numbers (blurred) with copy buttons, a green 本人確認 (9/9) badge and a red 繋がらない badge; and a 接触のヒント (参考) box with a green-outlined line about the 2026-09-18 contact. Callout labels overlay both screenshots. Lighting is flat UI, no photographs. Both images are fully framed inside their panels, not cropped to bleed.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark navy #0F172A. Dark navy background #0F172A with no gradient. Title off-white #F8FAFC in a bold geometric sans (Inter-like), tight tracking, about 6.0% of slide height, weight 700. Eyebrow 'PRODUCT — OPERATOR' and 'OPERATOR' labels in periwinkle #7C94EE, bold monospace, widely letter-spaced, about 2% of height. Column headings bold sans #F8FAFC at about 2.4%. Body copy grey-blue #94A3B8, regular sans, about 1.9%. Callout labels in dark blue #1E3A8A monospace, about 1.3%, on dark pills. Accent 'decision_log' in periwinkle #7C94EE monospace. Panels filled #1E293B with a white 10% overlay (effective light slate), thin 1px white 10% borders, small corner radius about 1% of width, no shadows. Title accent bar in #1E3A8A at the left of the title.

Two product screenshots, both boxed inside rounded dark panels, and no charts, logos or icons as separate items. **Design**

Background: Solid near-black navy #0F172A, flat with no pattern or photograph. Dark, minimal, tech-style slide with a near-black navy background. Title in bold geometric sans-serif (Inter-like) at about 6.0% of slide height, colour off-white #F8FAFC, with a short vertical accent bar in #1E3A8A at its left. Eyebrows 'DECISION TRAIL', 'REPORT — Monthly report' and 'TIMELINE — Decision log' are letter-spaced monospace capitals in periwinkle #7C94EE at about 1.8 to 2.0% of slide height. Subheadings are bold sans at about 2.4% in #F8FAFC; body text is regular sans at about 1.9% in slate grey #94A3B8. Accent colour for the quoted highlight 'answered every month, in writing' is #7C94EE. ### Slide 17

**Review notes**

- Suggested repair: Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.

**Text**

What you can say after 6 months
DELIVERABLES — Decision foundation in hand by month 6
DECISIONS — Decisions you can make by
month 6
Market health benchmarks
Conversation success rate, negative response rate, market NO-signal rate, residual rate — set
for your company, with every industry judged against the same thresholds
Market recovery rate
Measure how often once-negative prospects return to positive — quantify market elasticity
Validity of BDR investment
ROI computed from market health score and list-lifetime forecast
Winning patterns by industry / role
A/B test results turned into language for messaging, list, and role targeting
Explain to your boss with the decision report
A reason set for continue / stop / adjust
CONTINUE
続ける
Able to explain "why we continue" in
numbers and logic
ADJUST
変える
Able to articulate what to change — list, script,
or target
STOP
一旦退く
Able to explain "why we stop now" against a
defined exit threshold
Not just outcomes (meetings or revenue) — accountability for the decisions themselves, built in 6 months.
LOGOTORU OS
17 / 21

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a full-width dark slide: a left column of five stacked deliverable cards (x 5.0 to 53.4%) and a right column of three stacked decision cards (x 60.4 to 95.0%), separated by a centred arrow at x about 55%. The title sits top-left at y 10.0% with a thin blue accent bar at its left edge, left-aligned to the 5% margin; section labels sit above each column at y 19.6%. A full-width summary banner spans x 5.0 to 95.0% at y 84.2 to 91.1%. Footer text 'LOGOTORU OS' sits bottom-left at x 1.2% and the page number '17 / 21' bottom-right at x 94.0%, both at y 96.9%.

**Visuals**

Five deliverable cards in the left column, each a rounded rectangle filled #1E293B with a white 10% overlay and a thin light border, stacked with equal gaps: card 1 at y 23.7% (h 11.3%), card 2 at y 35.7% (h 11.2%), card 3 at y 47.7% (h 11.2%), card 4 at y 59.6% (h 11.3%), card 5 at y 71.6% (h 11.3%), each spanning x 5.0 to 53.4%. Between columns, a solid periwinkle right-pointing arrow glyph '→' (#7C94EE, about 6% of slide height) at x 55.2%, y 48.3%, pointing from the deliverables to the decisions. Three decision cards on the right, each spanning x 60.4 to 95.0% (w 34.6%, h about 18.2%): top card at y 26.5% with a #10B981 green tint (10% fill, 40% border); middle card at y 45.6% with a #F59E0B amber tint (10% fill, 40% border); bottom card at y 64.6% with an #EF4444 red tint (10% fill, 40% border). Each decision card holds a solid pill badge: green #10B981 badge reading 'CONTINUE' with Japanese '続ける' beneath it at x 62.6%, y 33.4%; amber #F59E0B badge reading 'ADJUST' with '変える' beneath it at x 62.6%, y 52.5%; red #EF4444 badge reading 'STOP' with '一旦退く' beneath it at x 62.6%, y 71.6%. The badge text is white bold sans-serif. No photographs, icons or logos are present.

**Design**

Background: Solid #0F172A dark navy (two full-slide #FFFFFF fills in the file are covered by it). Dark navy slide with a solid background of #0F172A. Title in near-white #F8FAFC, bold geometric sans-serif (close to Inter Bold), about 6.0% of slide height, tight tracking. Section labels in periwinkle #7C94EE, bold monospace with wide letter-spacing, about 1.85% of slide height, set in capitals for the first word. Card titles in #F8FAFC semibold sans-serif at about 2.2%; card body text in slate grey #94A3B8 regular sans-serif at about 1.8% to 1.9%. Accent colour is periwinkle #7C94EE, used for the arrow, section labels and the highlighted phrase in the banner. Cards use #1E293B fill with a 10% white overlay, thin subtle borders and small corner radii (about 0.5% of width). Decision cards have fills tinted by their status colour and 1px-equivalent borders at 40% opacity. Badges are solid-filled with small corner radii and white bold text. The bottom banner is a #1E293B rounded rectangle with small corner radius and no shadow. No gradients or shadows are visible.

### Slide 18

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Side-by-side month-by-month grids for the traditional and LOGOTORU scenarios make the timing of the exit decision easy to compare, with the hypothetical nature footnoted.
- Review: The readable slides 1, 11 and 18 are well built: slide 11's chart is annotated with a clear takeaway bar, and slide 18 carries one scenario comparison with honest hypothetical labels.
- Worth keeping: Keep the Continue/Adjust/Stop color system, the strong headline-plus-takeaway structure of slides 8, 9 and 11, and the scenario layout of slide 18 with its explicit hypothetical footnote.

**Text**

**Layout**

Single 16:9 slide with a full-width title at top-left, starting at about 5% left margin and sitting on a thin blue accent bar. Below it, a three-column stat strip spans the full width, then a two-column body: a left block of six month columns with two scenario rows (about 65% width) and a right column of two stacked scenario cards (about 23% width). A centred callout sits under the left block, a full-width rounded message bar sits below, and a right-aligned footnote follows; the footer has 'LOGOTORU OS' at bottom-left and page number '18 / 21' at bottom-right, with margins of about 1.2% left and 4% right.

**Visuals**

Not a chart or photograph. A schematic status grid: two rows (Traditional and LOGOTORU) by six month columns (Month 1 to Month 6) of rounded pill labels. Traditional row: six pills, all NEGATIVE, red text on dark red pills. LOGOTORU row: four NEGATIVE pills (months 1 to 4) and two PAUSED pills (months 5 and 6), the PAUSED pills in grey-slate with grey text. Right side: two bordered scenario cards. Card 1 (red, BURNED scenario): '5 accounts × ¥30M' then '= ¥150M' in large red type. Card 2 (green, PRESERVED scenario): '100 accounts × ¥30M' then '= ¥3B' in large green type. Top strip: three stat blocks, TARGETS 100 accounts, AT RISK 5 accounts (red), LTV ¥30M, separated by two thin vertical dividers. Glyphs ① and ② are circled-number characters used as markers; ↑ is an arrow glyph in the callout. No logos, icons, photos or illustrations.

**Design**

Background: Solid navy #0F172A (two full-slide #FFFFFF fills lie beneath it and are covered). Dark navy background #0F172A throughout with flat fills and no gradients or shadows. Title in bold geometric sans (Inter-like), #F8FAFC, about 6% of slide height, with a blue accent bar #1E40AF at its left. Body text in regular sans (Inter-like), #94A3B8 grey, about 1.6 to 1.8% of slide height. Labels and scenario headers in monospace (JetBrains Mono-like) with wide letter-spacing, in grey #94A3B8 and red #F87171 or green #34D399. Stat values bold sans, 3.5% of height, #F8FAFC except red #F87171 for 5 accounts. Accent colours: red #F87171 / #EF4444, green #34D399 / #10B981, amber #FBBF24 for the callout, periwinkle #7C94EE for LOGOTORU and the highlighted phrase. Panels and cards use #1E293B fills with rounded corners of about 8px; the scenario cards are tinted with red or green at 10% and outlined at 30% opacity. Dividers are thin slate lines.

### Slide 19

**Review notes**

- Review: But most of the contact sheet is dense, with body text far too small for a screen or room, such as the text-heavy slides 3, 4, 5, 6 and 19, and the product screenshots on 13, 14, 15 and 16 are small, mostly in Japanese and cut off, so an English reader cannot verify the product.
- Suggested repair: Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.

**Layout**

A five-column comparison table fills the lower two-thirds of the slide below a left-aligned title and a one-line subtitle, with the title and subtitle flush to a left margin of about 5% of the width. The first column holds the row labels, the next three hold the alternatives, and the rightmost column is the LOGOTORU column with a blue tint. The footer wordmark sits bottom-left and the page number bottom-right, both on a shared baseline near 97% of the height.

**Visuals**

One comparison table with 5 columns and 9 rows including the header. Columns: Dimension (row labels, left-aligned) | In-house log capture | BI / CRM upgrade | Generic sales outsourcing | LOGOTORU (tinted column, highlighted in blue). Rows with cell values: 1) Decision log accumulation: In-house log capture '× Collapses (person-dependent)'; BI / CRM upgrade '△ Separate design needed'; Generic sales outsourcing '× Not preserved'; LOGOTORU '◎ Bundled in'. 2) Exit decision: '× Threshold is personal'; '△ Slow (analysis request)'; '× Generally not done'; '◎ Reported every month'. 3) Observation data quality: '× Varies wildly by rep'; '△ SFA data only'; '× Count-focused'; '◎ Monthly verdict on 5 indicators + industry / role analysis'. 4) BDR execution: '○ In-house resources'; '× No involvement'; '◎ Volume-focused'; '○ Multi-channel / strategic'. 5) Decision-maker list: '△ Manual updates'; '× None'; '△ Targets only'; '◎ By-name provision'. 6) Board-report suitability: '△ Person-dependent reports'; '△ Dashboards only'; '× Meeting-count report'; '◎ Reasoning fully articulated'. 7) Time to ramp: 'Months+'; 'Half-year+'; 'Instant–2 weeks'; '2–4 weeks'. 8) Cost: 'Headcount (hidden)'; 'BI build + headcount'; '¥300–800K / mo (rough range)'; '¥800K+ / mo (~$5.2K USD)'. 9) Best fit: 'Strong ops culture + dedicated experts'; 'Mature data stack + analytics team'; 'Just need volume'; 'Heavy decision accountability — must explain decisions' (blue text). Symbol key: × red (#F87171) means a negative; △ amber (#FBBF24) means partial; ◎ green (#34D399) means positive; ○ grey (#94A3B8) means neutral. No photographs, charts, or logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark navy #0F172A. Dark navy slide with flat fills and no gradients or shadows. Title and emphasised LOGOTORU text in bright off-white and periwinkle blue; body text in slate grey; accents in blue, green, red and amber. Typefaces: title and table text in Inter (a neo-grotesque sans-serif); 'Dimension', 'Best fit' and the footer in a monospace face (DejaVu Sans Mono style) with wide letter-spacing. Title is bold, about 6.0% of slide height; subtitle and body are regular, about 1.9–2.1% of slide height; best-fit row text about 1.7%. Table cells are separated by thin slate hairlines (about 1px, #334155). Row bands alternate slightly between dark and lighter slate. Cell corners are square. The title has a vertical blue accent bar to its left.

### Slide 20

**Review notes**

- Suggested repair: Raise body text to a readable size and cut the copy on slides 3 to 6, 12, 17, 19 and 20, and show product screens large with a few callouts and English labels on slides 13 to 16.

**Text**

FAQ
Q. Won't bringing in an external party demoralize our in-house BDR team?
A. The opposite. LOGOTORU exists to show "doing more under these conditions is a business loss." We bring it in to free your team from inefficient activity.
Q. ¥800K+/mo is expensive. Aren't trade shows or ads better?
A. No setup fee. The execution plans include the decision-maker list and BDR strategy report at no extra cost (the Outbound Design Package, ¥500K, is the standalone option). Stopping at the right moment avoids losses of tens to hundreds of millions of yen — infrastructure for your whole marketing budget, not a one-off campaign.
Q. Couldn't we just enforce log entry on our internal BDR team and analyze it ourselves?
A. Theoretically yes. Practically very difficult — psychologically and structurally. Asking the people doing the work to objectively record "data that may reduce the need for their activity" carries a powerful bias.
Q. If we end up deciding to exit, isn't the BDR investment wasted?
A. On the contrary — it's the biggest return (loss avoidance). The tens of millions to hundreds of millions of yen in losses from hesitant continuation can be locked in and stopped. This "cutting losses" is itself one of the executive wins.
Q. Won't aggressive BDR execution come across as pushy and damage the brand?
A. Making that exact risk visible and controlling it is the essence of LOGOTORU. We continuously measure the "market NO-signal rate" (the share of people who have rejected you) and flag ADJUST at the caution line (5%) and STOP at the stop line (20%).
Q. We're not at the tightly-managed phase yet. Can we just wait and see?
A. Have you defined the deadline for "wait and see" and the criteria for the decision at that point? Continuing without those is not strategic patience — it is decision deferral.
LOGOTORU OS
20 / 21

**Layout**

Single slide with a title block at top left, followed by a two-column grid of six rounded cards (three rows, two columns, equal widths, about 5% side margins and a 2.7% gutter). The title FAQ sits top left at about 10% from the top, with a thin vertical blue accent bar to its left. Footer text sits at the bottom left and bottom right at about 97% from the top, with no slide-number logo; the page number reads 20 / 21 at right.

**Visuals**

None beyond the decorative elements: six flat rounded rectangular cards in #1E293B with a thin 1px light border (white at 10% opacity) and a small corner radius, and one thin vertical blue accent bar beside the title. No photographs, charts, icons or logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark navy #0F172A, full bleed. Dark navy full-bleed background #0F172A. Title FAQ in heavy bold sans-serif (Inter Bold style), #F8FAFC, about 6% of slide height. Card questions in semibold sans-serif (Inter SemiBold style), #F8FAFC, about 2.2% of height. Card answers in regular sans-serif (Inter Regular style), #94A3B8 slate grey, about 1.9% of height, with generous line spacing (about 3.1% of height between lines). Accent bar in royal blue #1D4ED8. Footer and page number in a letter-spaced monospace (Courier-like), about 1.6% of height, in white at reduced opacity (rendered as grey, about #475569). Cards flat, no shadows, no gradients.

### Slide 21

**Review notes**

- What would change the score: Rework slide 21 to match the deck's visual style and bring the pricing slide 6 into a simple, legible price ladder instead of a dense table with fine print.

**Text**

**Layout**

Two-column split: a white left panel covering the first 61.6% of the width holds the content, and a full-height dark navy right panel (38.4% width) holds the contact block. The left column is left-aligned on a margin of about 1.8% of the width, with the NEXT STEPS eyebrow at the top, a bordered offer card from 15.6% to 44.4% height, a light info strip from 46.9% to 64.7%, and a COMPANY INFO key-value block from 67.6% to 89.6%. The right column is left-aligned at 62.5% with the contact label at the top, the name below it, three stacked contact rows, a divider near 78% height, a bold tagline and body text at the bottom, then a logo at the left and the page number '21 / 21' at the right, both near 97% height. There is no visible slide-level title; the top-left NEXT STEPS eyebrow acts as the section label.

**Visuals**

Visual elements: (1) A QR code at the upper right of the offer card, roughly 7.3% wide by 12.6% high in black modules on white, inside a white square box with a thin light grey border (#E2E8F0), centred over the caption SCAN ME; it links to the booking page. (2) Three line icons in the right panel, drawn in thin strokes: an envelope outline (light grey #E2E8F0) beside the email address, a globe with meridians (periwinkle #7C94EE) beside the URL, and a calendar outline with binding tabs (periwinkle #7C94EE) beside the booking row. (3) A small star glyph in the RECOMMENDED badge. (4) Circled numerals 1, 2 and 3 in periwinkle #7C94EE marking the three call steps, with small light grey #CBD5E1 arrows after steps 1 and 2. (5) A small white-on-dark LOGOTORU wordmark at the bottom-left of the right panel. No photographs, charts or diagrams.

**Design**

Background: Split solid background: white #FFFFFF on the left 61.6% and dark navy #0F172A on the right 38.4%. Two-tone split: white (#FFFFFF) left panel and deep navy (#0F172A) right panel. Accent colour is royal navy #1E3A8A for the card border, badge, button and tinted fills; periwinkle #7C94EE for eyebrows, the URL, the booking link and step numerals. Headline text is near-black navy #0F172A; body copy is slate #475569 with darker slate #334155 for values; labels and captions are #94A3B8 and #64748B. On the dark panel, the name and tagline are #F8FAFC and the body is #94A3B8. Typefaces: body and headlines in a neo-grotesque sans-serif (Inter-like), bold for the title and name and regular for body; section labels, contact rows and the badge in a monospace face (DejaVu Sans Mono or JetBrains Mono-like) with wide letter-spacing and uppercase. Title text is about 3.4% of slide height (cap height about 2.4%); the contact name is about 3.9%; body text is about 2.1%; footnotes and captions are about 1.4 to 1.9%. Shapes use small corner radii (about 0.5 to 1% of width), a 2px-equivalent navy stroke on the card, and no shadows or gradients. Contact rows are filled rectangles with slightly rounded corners.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)
